# Complex Matrix Multiplication Below 2.258 and Rectangular Bounds

Coordinate `EN-114-000-0022-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-B-ML-M-0234` | `PM-107.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Complex-Matrix-Multiplication-Below-2.258-and-Rectangular-Bounds-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint Complex-Matrix-Multiplication-Below-2.258-and-Rectangular-Bounds-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). ```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "— secondary writeup\n\nOver every field of characteristic zero, we prove that the square matrix-multiplication exponent satisfies <i>ω</i> &lt; 2.258, the dual exponent satisfies <i>α</i> &gt; 0.465, and $`\\omega(1,0.709,1)\\lt 2.092`$. The strict square and <i>k</i> = 0.709 rectangular bounds also hold over every field except possibly in one finite set of positive characteristics, in the arithmetic-operation model.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> — secondary writeup
> 
> Over every field of characteristic zero, we prove that the square matrix-multiplication exponent satisfies <i>ω</i> &lt; 2.258, the dual exponent satisfies <i>α</i> &gt; 0.465, and $`\omega(1,0.709,1)\lt 2.092`$. The strict square and <i>k</i> = 0.709 rectangular bounds also hold over every field except possibly in one finite set of positive characteristics, in the arithmetic-operation model.

- **claim-main-exponents** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-rectangular)): Over $\mathbb C$, the arithmetic matrix-multiplication exponents satisfy $$\omega<\frac{1129}{500}=2.258,\qquad \alpha>0.465,\qquad w(0.709)<2.092.$$
  - Explanation: Over the complex numbers the square, dual and rectangular exponents satisfy the three strict bounds of the main theorem.
- **claim-rank-exponent-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-exponent)): For $a_i\ge0$, $$\label{eq:rank-exponent} W(a_1,a_2,a_3)= \lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{\log\mathcal R (\lceil e^{sa_1}\rceil,\lceil e^{sa_2}\rceil, \lceil e^{sa_3}\rceil)}{s}.$$ Moreover, $W$ is coordinatewise nondecreasing, homogeneous, subadditive, permutation invariant, and continuous. It satisfies $$W(a_1,a_2,a_3)\ge\max_{i\ne j}(a_i+a_j).$$
  - Explanation: The exponent is a limit of normalized log ranks and has the listed structural properties.
- **claim-border-overhead** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-border-overhead)): For a fixed tensor of cost $R$, its $N$-th tensor power has exact rank at most $O(N+1)R^N$, where the implicit constant may depend on the fixed witness.
  - Explanation: Interpolation converts a witness of fixed cost into an exact rank bound for tensor powers with linear overhead.
- **claim-equal-rectangular-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rectangular-sum)): Suppose a tensor of cost $R$ restricts or degenerates to a direct sum of $M$ copies of the matrix-multiplication tensor with dimensions $l_1,l_2,l_3$. Then $$\label{eq:rectangular-sum} W(\log l_1,\log l_2,\log l_3)\le \log R-\log M.$$
  - Explanation: Degeneration to equal matrix-product summands bounds the exponent by log cost minus log multiplicity.
- **claim-cw-border-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-cw-rank)): The tensor $T$ has border rank three. Consequently $\mathop{\mathrm{\underline R}}(T^{\otimes n})\leq3^n$ for every integer $n\geq1$.
  - Explanation: The six-term Coppersmith-Winograd tensor has border rank three, hence powers have border rank at most the matching power of three.
- **claim-asymptotic-sum-inequality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-asi)): If a tensor $U$ degenerates to $\bigoplus_{\ell=1}^{L}\langle a_\ell,b_\ell,c_\ell\rangle$, with all dimensions positive integers, then $$\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}(a_\ell b_\ell c_\ell)^{\omega/3} \leq\mathop{\mathrm{\underline R}}(U).$$ In particular, if $T^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal A\unrhd \bigoplus_{\ell=1}^{L}\langle a,b,c\rangle$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{\underline R}}(\mathcal A)\leq R$, then $$L(abc)^{\omega/3}\leq3^nR. \label{alg:asi-account}$$
  - Explanation: Schönhage's asymptotic sum inequality in border-rank form.
- **claim-pairwise-separation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-separator)): Let $U=\sum_{s=1}^{K}U_s$, where $U_s$ uses $X$-variables in $X_s$ and $Y$-variables in $Y_s$, and the families $(X_s)_{s=1}^K$ and $(Y_s)_{s=1}^K$ are each disjoint. The $Z$-variables may overlap. There are a finite abelian group $G$, its group multiplication tensor $\mathcal A_G$, and a positive integer $m$ such that $$U\otimes\mathcal A_G\unrhd \bigoplus_{s=1}^{K}\bigl(U_s\otimes\langle1,m,1\rangle\bigr). \label{alg:group-identity}$$ The degeneration is a zeroing-out on auxiliary coordinates controlled by the original local variables. It preserves all original coordinates and coefficients. For $K\to\infty$, the parameters can be chosen with $$\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(\mathcal A_G)=\mathop{\mathrm{\underline R}}(\mathcal A_G)=\|G\|, \qquad \log\|G\|=\log K+O(\sqrt{\log K}), \qquad m=K. \label{alg:group-rates}$$ The same statement holds for any other pair of knowing sides.
  - Explanation: A group multiplication tensor and local projections separate labels known on two sides into a direct sum of pairings.
- **claim-fiber-preservation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-payload)): The identity in Lemma <a href="#lem:group" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:group">[lem:group]</a> holds with arbitrary coefficients and arbitrary unresolved local indices inside the tensors $U_s$, including indices correlated between different original occurrences. Local support projections and monomial leading-term restrictions have the same property. Finite compositions of these operations may leave all previously produced matrix multiplication factors untouched.
  - Explanation: The separation identity acts coefficientwise and so preserves arbitrary unresolved correlated fibers.
- **claim-label-hierarchy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hierarchy)): Let $b\geq1$, and let $\mathcal B\subseteq\mathcal S^b$ be nonempty. Suppose local restrictions and degenerations of $T^{\otimes b}$ retain exactly these original scalar components, with their original coordinates and coefficients. Suppose there are finitely many labels $S_1,\ldots,S_t$, each a function of a component in $\mathcal B$, with these properties:* 1. *the complete record $(S_1,\ldots,S_t)$ determines the original component;* 2. *after the earlier labels are fixed, the next label is independently readable by two fixed sides within that conditional pool;* 3. *every component designated by a retained complete record survives all the local restrictions.* *Let $\alpha$ be a rational probability distribution on $\mathcal B$. For admissible $N\to\infty$, there are auxiliary tensors $\mathcal A_N$ and degenerations of $T^{\otimes bN}\otimes\mathcal A_N$ into $L_N$ independent matrix multiplication tensors of common volume $v_N$, with $\mathop{\mathrm{\underline R}}(\mathcal A_N)\leq R_N$, such that $$\log L_N=NH(\alpha)+o(N),\qquad \log R_N=NH(\alpha)+o(N),\qquad \log v_N=NH(\alpha)+o(N). \label{alg:hierarchy-rates}$$ When $H(\alpha)>0$, this gives $\omega\leq3b\log3/H(\alpha)$.
  - Explanation: A finite hierarchy of two-sided readable labels gives multiplicity, rank and volume of entropy order.
- **claim-completion-degeneration** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-completion)): Let a tensor have a W-partition with labels $0,1,2$. For an integer $b\geq1$, a local monomial degeneration of its $b$-th power retains exactly the coarse words $$\{0,1\}^b\ \cup\ \{0,2\}^b. \label{alg:complete-support}$$ This support has a new W-partition: the constant word $0^b$, the words containing $1$, and the words containing $2$. Conditional on either nonzero new label, the original binary word is independently readable by two sides. The construction preserves arbitrary unresolved fibers.
  - Explanation: A local monomial degeneration keeps words with at most one nonzero label and yields a new W-partition.
- **claim-elementary-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-elementary)): For every integer $b\geq2$, $$\omega\leq \frac{3b\log3}{\log(4^b+3^b-2^{b+1})}. \label{alg:complete-bound}$$ In particular, $$\omega\leq\frac{9\log3}{\log75}=2.290107196\ldots.$$
  - Explanation: Completion of blocks and separation of the retained components give an explicit square exponent bound per block length.
- **claim-parity-restriction** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-parity)): In three W-partitioned occurrences, a local monomial degeneration removes exactly the six words in which all three labels occur and retains the other 21 coarse words. On the retained support, the majority label and the odd-count label each define a W-partition. Once both labels are direct, the remaining choice is either deterministic or independently readable by two sides. All statements remain true with arbitrary unresolved fibers.
  - Explanation: Parity removes the words with all three labels and yields majority and odd-count W-partitions.
- **claim-orientation-supplies** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-orientation-banks)): Fix a finite oriented schedule of operations with prescribed exact conditional populations. Suppose its exact types fix, for each relative physical permutation $\tau\in\mathfrak S_3$, the canonical input histogram $H_\tau$ of all original occurrences with that permutation, independently of the direct history. Include all six global physical side permutations of this schedule, with the same prescribed populations and histograms in corresponding replicas. Then every original orientation bank has the same exact canonical input histogram. Auxiliary supplies and pairing dimensions are independent of the direct history. All regroupings controlled by preceding direct labels are implementable by local block-diagonal maps.
  - Explanation: Replicating a schedule in all six side permutations equalizes canonical histograms across banks.
- **claim-completion-tree-hierarchy** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-completion-hierarchy)): Consider a finite rooted tree whose leaves are W-partitioned occurrences and whose internal nodes apply Lemma <a href="#alg:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:completion">10</a> to their children. Before a child is used at a parent, allow a fixed physical permutation of that whole child block. The retained support has a W-partition at its root. Once the root label is direct, the original leaf-label word admits a finite hierarchy of pairwise-readable refinements. All refinements preserve arbitrary unresolved leaf fibers.* *In a fixed root fiber, let $P$ be any rational law on the retained original leaf-label words. Across $N$ copies of that fiber, exact conditional types and Lemma <a href="#lem:group" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:group">[lem:group]</a> give direct multiplicity, auxiliary rank bound, and added pairing volume whose logarithms are each $NH(P)+o(N)$. The auxiliary product and pairing dimensions are common to every direct output. Canonical original labels are unchanged by the intermediate physical permutations.
  - Explanation: A tree of completion gates admits a hierarchy of pairwise-readable refinements with entropy-rate resources.
- **claim-recipe-composition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-recipe-composition)): Consider a primitive completion, a finite tree of completion gates as in Lemma <a href="#alg:completion-hierarchy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:completion-hierarchy">14</a>, or a parity operation on $b$ original occurrences. Let $s$ be its outer W label, with a rational law $a_s$, separated by a fixed recipe tree with limiting score $u_0$. In the outer group $s$, let $t$ be a further W label, with conditional rational law $r_{st}$, separated by a fixed recipe tree with limiting score $u_s$. For completion this label is deterministic and $u_s=0$. For parity it is the other of the majority and odd-count labels. For each permitted $(s,t)$, choose a rational distribution $P_{st}$ on its remaining original coarse words. If $n_i(w)$ counts canonical input label $i$ in the word $w$, then the resulting original law and limiting score are $$\begin{aligned} q_i&=\frac1b\sum_{s,t}a_s r_{st}\, \mathbb E_{w\sim P_{st}}n_i(w), \label{alg:recipe-law}\\ u&=\frac1b\left(u_0+\sum_s a_su_s +\sum_{s,t}a_s r_{st}H(P_{st})\right). \label{alg:recipe-score} \end{aligned}$$ The formula is feasible for every stipulated finite collection of child recipes and rational distributions; no optimality assertion is required.
  - Explanation: The resulting law and limiting score of a completion, tree or parity recipe are given by explicit averaging formulas.
- **claim-value-bounds-concavity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-values)): For $0<\beta\le3$ and $\gamma\ge1$, $$0\le F_\beta(\alpha)\le H(\alpha)\le\log6, \qquad 0\le U^W_\gamma(q)\le\gamma H(q)\le\gamma\log3. \label{val:entropy-cap}$$ Both functions are concave and continuous on their closed probability simplices. They have supporting affine functionals at every relative interior point.
  - Explanation: The attainable-value functions are entropy-capped, concave and continuous with supporting functionals.
- **claim-canonical-symmetry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-val-canonical-symmetry)): For $\tau\in\mathfrak S_3$, define $(\tau_*\alpha)(s)=\alpha(\tau^{-1}s)$. Then $$F_\beta(\tau_*\alpha)=F_\beta(\alpha),\qquad U^W_\gamma(\tau_*q)=U^W_\gamma(q).$$
  - Explanation: The value functions are invariant under permuting canonical laws by side permutations.
- **claim-exact-law-correction** (construction, [source](#stmt-val-dilution)): If a construction or a convex mixture has law $\widehat\alpha$, and a desired law $\alpha$ has positive coordinates, put $$\lambda=\max_{s:\widehat\alpha_s>0} \left(1-\frac{\alpha_s}{\widehat\alpha_s}\right)_+.$$ For $\lambda>0$, the vector $r=(\alpha-(1-\lambda)\widehat\alpha)/\lambda$ is a probability law. Mixing the construction with singleton constructions of average law $r$ therefore gives $\alpha$ exactly, at loss at most $K\lambda$ if the old score is at most $K$, where $K\ge0$. Moreover $$\lambda\le \max_s\frac{(\widehat\alpha_s-\alpha_s)_+}{\alpha_s}.$$ When the data are rational, this is a finite rational time-sharing operation. For limiting data it is justified by the defining closure. This correction will be used when converting approximate finite laws to exact target laws.
  - Explanation: Mixing with singleton constructions corrects an approximate law to an exact one at a bounded loss.
- **claim-recompile** (theorem, [source](#stmt-val-recompile)): Fix a finite recipe on $n$ original occurrences with canonical law $q$, $L$ terminal labels, charged auxiliary rank $R$, and additional volume $v$. Fix $\gamma\ge1$. There is a sequence of recipes on larger exact banks, with the same canonical law $q$, such that $$\liminf u_\gamma^{\mathrm{new}} \ge \frac{\gamma\log L-\gamma\log R+\log v}{n}, \qquad \liminf(D^{\mathrm{new}}-A^{\mathrm{new}})\ge0. \label{val:recompile-goal}$$ All source-word restrictions of the old recipe hold separately in every old macroblock of the new recipes.
  - Explanation: Recompiling separation calls on long pools keeps the law and removes the normalized rank excess.
- **claim-gamma-monotonicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-monotonicity)): For $1\le\gamma_0\le\gamma_1$ and every $\mathsf W$ law $q$, $$U^W_{\gamma_1}(q)\ge U^W_{\gamma_0}(q).$$
  - Explanation: The three-state value does not decrease in the formal parameter.
- **claim-progression-free-labels** (construction, [source](#stmt-val-ap-set)): For all sufficiently large integers $L$ there is a set $J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,L\}$ containing no nonconstant three-term arithmetic progression and satisfying $$\log\|J\|=\log L-O(\sqrt{\log L}).$$ For primes $P\to\infty$, a subset of $\mathbb F_P$ of size $P\exp(-O(\sqrt{\log P}))$ has the same property for progressions modulo $P$.
  - Explanation: Behrend's sphere construction gives large progression-free sets of integers and residues.
- **claim-marginal-matching** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-matching)): Let $\alpha\in\Delta(\mathcal S)$, and let $\alpha_X,\alpha_Y,\alpha_Z$ be its three canonical local-grade marginals. For every $0<\beta\le3$, $$F_\beta(\alpha)\ge H_{\mathrm{marg}}(\alpha) :=\frac{H(\alpha_X)+H(\alpha_Y)+H(\alpha_Z)}3. \label{val:matching-bound}$$ The constructions proving this bound are strong realizations with no auxiliary input and no additional matrix multiplication volume.
  - Explanation: Hash filtering gives a lower bound equal to the average marginal entropy without auxiliary tensor.
- **claim-face-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-face)): Suppose $\alpha=(p_0,p_1,p_2,E_0,E_1,E_2)\in\Delta(\mathcal S)$ satisfies $E_1=0$. Put $$\begin{aligned} Q&=(p_2+E_0,\ p_0+E_2,\ p_1),\label{val:face-Q}\\ J(\alpha)&=\mathop{\mathrm{en}}(p_2)+\mathop{\mathrm{en}}(E_0)+\mathop{\mathrm{en}}(p_0)+\mathop{\mathrm{en}}(E_2) -\mathop{\mathrm{en}}(Q_0)-\mathop{\mathrm{en}}(Q_1),\label{val:face-J} \end{aligned}$$ where $\mathop{\mathrm{en}}(s)=-s\log s$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{en}}(0)=0$. Then for $0<\beta\le3$, $$F_\beta(\alpha)\ge \frac\beta3\bigl(U^W_{3/\beta}(Q)+J(\alpha)\bigr). \label{val:face-bound}$$ In particular, if $1\le\gamma_0\le3/\beta$, the same lower bound holds with $U^W_{\gamma_0}$ in place of $U^W_{3/\beta}$.
  - Explanation: On the face the six-state value is bounded below by the three-state value plus an explicit conditional entropy.
- **claim-lower-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-ap)): Fix $0<\beta\leq3$, and let $\alpha(\mu)$ be a $C^2$ chart of strictly positive six-state laws, parametrized by their means on an open subset of $\{\mu:\sum_i\mu_i=2\}$. Suppose that a $C^2$ function $\varphi$ touches $F_\beta\circ\alpha$ from below at an interior point $\mu_*$. If $g_*$ is any supporting supergradient of $F_\beta$ at $\alpha(\mu_*)$, then $$\label{ap:lower-test} \mathcal L\varphi(\mu_*) \leq g_*\cdot\mathcal L\alpha(\mu_*)-1.$$
  - Explanation: A smooth function touching the six-state value from below satisfies a covariance-contraction inequality.
- **claim-integer-progression-free** (construction, [source](#stmt-ap-behrend)): For all sufficiently large integers $L$, there is a set $J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,L\}$ with no nonconstant three-term arithmetic progression and $$\log\|J\|\geq\log L-O(\sqrt{\log L}).$$
  - Explanation: The integer progression-free set supplies the shared target labels.
- **claim-compile-tree** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-compile)): Fix a finite horizon $n$, a finite set of disjoint shared terminal windows $J$, and a rational target law $a$. At every target and prefix node $v=(j,h)$, prescribe an earlier finite strong realization with exact canonical law $q_v$, utility $u_v$, and uniform auxiliary and byproduct dimensions. Assume that every path permitted by these laws belongs to its target windows. Put $$w_{j,h}=a_j\prod_{t=1}^{\|h\|}q_{j,h_{<t}}(h_t), \qquad \overline q=\frac1n\sum_{\|h\|<n,\ j\in J}w_{j,h}q_{j,h}.$$ Then finite strong realizations with exact law $\overline q$ attain, in the limit of their outer bank sizes, $$\label{ap:compile-score} u_{\mathrm{out}} \geq\frac{\lambda H(a)}n +\frac1n\sum_{\|h\|<n,\ j\in J}w_{j,h}u_{j,h}.$$ The limiting notation only removes a multinomial type-count loss; every member of the family is a finite tensor construction using one fixed auxiliary tensor and nonzero counted outputs.
  - Explanation: A finite control tree of strong realizations compiles into one realization with the weighted utility plus target entropy.
- **claim-corridor-control** (construction, [source](#stmt-ap-corridor)): Assume that every $\|m\|\leq m_0$ is available as a soft mean, with cost at most $\|m\|^2/2$, bounded increments $\|X\|\leq B$, and the limit <a href="#ap:noise-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ap:noise-limit">[ap:noise-limit]</a>. Assume also the singleton controls above. Fix $c>0$ and a target bound $C_y>0$. For every $\eta>0$ there is a constant $K_\eta$, independent of $L$, with the following property. For each integer $L\geq2$, all sufficiently large finite horizons $n$, and every target $y$ with $\|y\|\leq C_y\sqrt n$, there is a finite control tree such that every permitted path satisfies $$\left\|\sum_{t=1}^n X_t-y\right\|<w, \qquad w=c\sqrt n/L,$$ and its expected total cost is at most $$\label{ap:total-cost} \mathbb E\sum_{t=1}^n c_t\leq(\tau+2\eta)\log L+K_\eta.$$ The expectation is only an accounting device for the tree’s transition laws; the terminal inclusion holds for every path.
  - Explanation: A shrinking-corridor control sends every permitted path into the terminal window at logarithmic expected cost.
- **claim-w-lower-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-w-ap)): Fix $\gamma\geq1$. On the relative interior of the three-letter simplex, put $$C_W(q)=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(q)-qq^\top, \qquad \mathcal L_W=C_W(q):D^2.$$ Every $C^2$ lower test $\psi$ for $U_\gamma$ satisfies $$\label{ap:w-test} \mathcal L_W\psi\leq-\gamma$$ at its contact point.
  - Explanation: Every smooth lower test for the three-state value satisfies the contraction inequality against the negative parameter.
- **claim-strict-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ap-comparison)): Let $\Omega$ be a bounded domain in the mean plane with compact closure, and let a chart $\alpha$ as in Theorem <a href="#thm:ap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:ap">26</a> extend continuously to $\overline\Omega$. Suppose $f\in C(\overline\Omega)\cap C^2(\Omega)$, $f\leq F_\beta\circ\alpha$ on $\partial\Omega$, and, for every $\mu\in\Omega$ and every supporting supergradient $g$ there, $$\mathcal L f(\mu)>g\cdot\mathcal L\alpha(\mu)-1.$$ Then $f\leq F_\beta\circ\alpha$ throughout $\overline\Omega$. For the affine W chart, the analogous conclusion holds for $U_\gamma$ when $\mathcal L_W f>-\gamma$.
  - Explanation: A strict differential supersolution below the value on the boundary stays below it everywhere.
- **claim-chart-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-ap-chart-error)): Suppose $0\leq h\leq K$ is a concave value on the full source simplex, $\alpha$ is an interior law, and $g$ is a supporting supergradient there. For a probability-law chart through $\alpha$, $$\|g\cdot\mathcal L\alpha\| \leq K\max_i\frac{\|\mathcal L\alpha_i\|}{\alpha_i}.$$ In particular, if $\alpha_i\geq m>0$ and $\\|\mathcal L\alpha\\|_\infty\leq\varepsilon$, the right side is at most $K\varepsilon/m$.
  - Explanation: Concavity controls the supporting-plane term by the relative size of the chart operator.
- **claim-law-correction** (bound, [source](#stmt-cert-w-correction)): Let $q$ be a strictly positive probability vector, let $K\ge0$, and suppose a construction has law $\widehat q$ and attainable utility $u\le K$. Then the closed attainable value satisfies $$U(q)\ge u-K\max_i(\widehat q_i-q_i)_+/q_i.$$ For rational laws, the correction is realized by finite rational time-sharing; for arbitrary real $q$, the assertion concerns the defining closure.
  - Explanation: A construction with an approximate law gives a lower bound at an exact positive law with an explicit loss.
- **claim-w-seed** (bound, [source](#stmt-cert-w-seed)): With the preceding exact coefficients and domain, $$U(q)\ge F_0(q)+H_W(z)-9\cdot10^{-6}\quad (z\in\partial\Omega_W),$$ and $$U(q)\ge F_0(q)+H_W(z)-22\cdot10^{-6}\quad (z\in\Omega_W). \label{cert:w-interior-seed}$$
  - Explanation: The three-state value dominates the entropy baseline plus the polynomial correction up to small losses.
- **claim-certificate-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-certificate)): Let $\beta=1129/500$. The construction class defining $F_\beta$ satisfies $$F_\beta(\alpha_*)\ \geq\ 1.098619383270680538 \ >\ \log 3,$$ where $\alpha_*=(p,p,p,1/3-p,1/3-p,1/3-p)$ and $$p=2^{-500}\left\lfloor 2^{500}\frac{18623}{100000} +\frac12\right\rfloor.$$
  - Explanation: At the central law the six-state value strictly exceeds the logarithm of three.
- **claim-covariance-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ap-covariance)): For $\alpha=(p_0,p_1,p_2,E_0,E_1,E_2)$, set $$r_i=1-\mu_i,\qquad z=x+iy=3r_0-1+i\sqrt3(r_2-r_1),\qquad D_i=r_i(1-r_i)+2p_i.$$ Then $D_i=\operatorname{Var}(g_i)$. In the $(x,y)$ coordinates the covariance contraction has matrix $$\label{cert:operator} \begin{equation}\label{ap:real-covariance} C=\begin{pmatrix}9D_0&3\sqrt3(D_1-D_2)\\ 3\sqrt3(D_1-D_2)&-3D_0+6D_1+6D_2\end{pmatrix}. \end{equation} Writing \[ d=\frac{D_0+D_1+D_2}{3},\qquad f=\frac{D_0+\zeta D_1+\zeta^2D_2}{3}, \] the operator is \begin{equation}\label{ap:complex-operator} \mathcal L u =36\bigl(d\,u_{z\bar z}+f\,u_{zz} +\bar f\,u_{\bar z\bar z}\bigr). \end{equation}$$
  - Explanation: The covariance contraction in complex coordinates is expressed through the grade variances.
- **claim-interior-certificate** (bound, [source](#stmt-cert-interior-lemma)): On $\overline\Omega$, $$\mathcal L\varphi>-1+9.8\cdot10^{-8}.$$
  - Explanation: Interval verification shows the barrier satisfies the strict interior inequality.
- **claim-boundary-margin** (bound, [source](#stmt-cert-boundary-lemma)): On the boundary of the full six-state probability domain, $$F_\beta(\alpha(z))\geq\varphi(z)+5.5\cdot10^{-6}.$$
  - Explanation: On the boundary the attainable value exceeds the barrier by a uniform margin.
- **claim-entropy-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-entropy)): If a tensor of cost $e^C$ has a readable label tree, then $$\label{eq:entropy-bound} W(r_{XY}(\pi),r_{XZ}(\pi),r_{YZ}(\pi))\le C$$ for every leaf law $\pi$.
  - Explanation: A readable label tree bounds the exponent at the accumulated reader rates by the log cost.
- **claim-balancing** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-balance)): Fix a side $L$. Let $S$ be the sum of the two rates incident to $L$, and let $T$ be the remaining rate. Then $$\label{eq:balanced-rate} W(S/2,T,S/2)\le C.$$ For $C>0$, the normalized pairs $(S/C,T/C)$ from different constructions may be combined convexly and decreased coordinatewise while retaining the normalized bound.
  - Explanation: Averaging over the incident axes gives a symmetric bound and allows convex combination of normalized rate pairs.
- **claim-completion-local** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-completion)): Completion is a local leading-term degeneration. It produces another three-flag tensor with conditional readable trees. If the old tensor contains $b$ base factors of budget $R$, the new one has $mb$ such factors and budget $R^{mb}$.
  - Explanation: Completion is a local degeneration preserving three-flag structure and readable trees.
- **claim-score-optimality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-score-optimality)): The values in <a href="#eq:initial-scores" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:initial-scores">[eq:initial-scores]</a>–<a href="#eq:termination-score" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:termination-score">[eq:termination-score]</a> are the exact maxima of $S+tT$ over their conditional or complete leaf laws. Thus $F$ is the support function, in direction $(1,t)$, of the downward convex hull of the normalized rate pairs $(S/(b\log R),T/(b\log R))$ from $\mathcal F_7$.
  - Explanation: The recursive score formulas are exact maxima, so the support function describes the rate hull.
- **claim-shape-optimization** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-shape-optimization)): For $U=F$ or $U=F_p$, and $0\le k\le1$, the largest feasible scale on this ray is $$\label{eq:radial-optimum} d_U(k):=\max\{d\ge0:(2d,kd)\in\mathcal H_U\} =\min_{0\le t\le1}\frac{U(t)}{2+kt}.$$ Consequently, $$\label{eq:functional-bound} w(k)\le\frac1{d_U(k)} =\max_{0\le t\le1}\frac{2+kt}{U(t)}.$$ A maximizing parameter is the leftmost $t\in[0,1]$ for which $$\label{eq:contact-rule} (2+kt)U'_+(t)\ge kU(t),$$ or $t=1$ if no such point exists.
  - Explanation: For each aspect ratio the best bound is a one-parameter maximization with a contact rule.
- **claim-uniform-update** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-update)): The maintained properties are preserved by the following laws. At a completion centered on $\mathsf B$ of size $m$, $$\begin{aligned} \rho'&=\rho^m,& h'_{\mathsf B}&=m h_{\mathsf B}, \label{eq:minus-rho}\\ h'_i&= m\left(\frac{\rho-\rho'}{1-\rho'}h_{\mathsf B} +\frac{1-\rho}{1-\rho'}h_i\right) &&(i=\mathsf A,\mathsf C). \label{eq:minus-h} \end{aligned}$$ At a completion centered on $i\in\{\mathsf A,\mathsf C\}$, with $j$ the other inactive color, $$\begin{aligned} \rho'&=1-(1-\rho)^m,& h'_{\mathsf B}&= m\left(\frac{\rho}{\rho'}h_{\mathsf B} +\frac{\rho'-\rho}{\rho'}h_i\right), \label{eq:plus-rho}\\ h'_i&=mh_i,& h'_j&=\log\left[(e^{h_i}+e^{h_j})^m-e^{mh_i}\right]. \label{eq:plus-h} \end{aligned}$$ In both cases $b'=mb$.
  - Explanation: Explicit recurrences preserve uniformity of the first-side coordinate under completions.
- **claim-plane-preservation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-plane-preservation)): The condition $e\ge P_p(a,b,c)$ is preserved by all the operations in <a href="#def:baseline" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:baseline">48</a>. The square input satisfies it with equality, and the naive estimates satisfy it.
  - Explanation: The separating-plane inequality is preserved by all operations generating the baseline.
- **claim-baseline-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-comparison)): Assume $2<p\le2.258$ and $w(1)\le p$. Against $\mathcal B_p$, the new bounds are strictly better for $0.321334<k<1$. More generally they improve any separator-satisfying baseline throughout the part of $0<k<1$ on which that baseline exceeds two.
  - Explanation: The new bounds strictly improve the table baseline above its exponent-two endpoint.
- **claim-extra-inputs** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-extra-inputs)): Under <a href="#eq:old-envelope-assumption" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:old-envelope-assumption">[eq:old-envelope-assumption]</a>, every such additional input satisfies the separator test.
  - Explanation: Under the envelope assumptions any additional old input satisfies the separator test.
- **claim-characteristic-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-characteristic-transfer)): There is a finite set $\mathcal S$ of positive primes such that, for every field $F$ with $\operatorname{char}F=0$ or $\operatorname{char}F=p\notin\mathcal S$, $$\omega_F<\frac{1129}{500}=2.258,\qquad w_F\!\left(\frac{709}{1000}\right)<\frac{523}{250}=2.092.$$ The same set $\mathcal S$ works for both inequalities and depends on two fixed exact rank schemes selected in the proof.
  - Explanation: The strict square and rectangular bounds transfer to fields outside one finite set of positive characteristics.
- **claim-characteristic-zero-dual** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-characteristic-zero-dual)): For every field $F$ of characteristic zero, $\alpha_F>0.465$.
  - Explanation: The dual exponent bound holds over every field of characteristic zero.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hierarchy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b\\geq1$, and let $\\mathcal B\\subseteq\\mathcal S^b$ be nonempty. Suppose local restrictions and degenerations of $T^{\\otimes b}$ retain exactly these original scalar components, with their original coordinates and coefficients. Suppose there are finitely many labels $S_1,\\ldots,S_t$, each a function of a component in $\\mathcal B$, with these properties:*\n\n1.  *the complete record $(S_1,\\ldots,S_t)$ determines the original component;*\n\n2.  *after the earlier labels are fixed, the next label is independently readable by two fixed sides within that conditional pool;*\n\n3.  *every component designated by a retained complete record survives all the local restrictions.*\n\n\n*Let $\\alpha$ be a rational probability distribution on $\\mathcal B$. For admissible $N\\to\\infty$, there are auxiliary tensors $\\mathcal A_N$ and degenerations of $T^{\\otimes bN}\\otimes\\mathcal A_N$ into $L_N$ independent matrix multiplication tensors of common volume $v_N$, with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{\\underline R}}(\\mathcal A_N)\\leq R_N$, such that $$\\log L_N=NH(\\alpha)+o(N),\\qquad\n \\log R_N=NH(\\alpha)+o(N),\\qquad\n \\log v_N=NH(\\alpha)+o(N).\n \\label{alg:hierarchy-rates}$$ When $H(\\alpha)>0$, this gives $\\omega\\leq3b\\log3/H(\\alpha)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-107.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Consider a finite rooted tree whose leaves are W-partitioned occurrences and whose internal nodes apply Lemma <a href=\"#alg:completion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"alg:completion\">10</a> to their children. Once the root label is direct, the original leaf-label word admits a finite hierarchy of pairwise-readable refinements.         "anchor": "stmt-alg-recipe-composition",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a primitive completion, a finite tree of completion gates as in Lemma <a href=\"#alg:completion-hierarchy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"alg:completion-hierarchy\">14</a>, or a parity operation on $b$ original occurrences. Let $s$ be its outer W label, with a rational law $a_s$, separated by a fixed recipe tree with limiting score $u_0$. In the outer group $s$, let $t$ be a further W label, with conditional rational law $r_{st}$, separated by a fixed recipe tree with limiting score $u_s$. For completion this label is deterministic and $u_s=0$. For parity it is the other of the majority and odd-count labels. For each permitted $(s,t)$, choose a rational distribution $P_{st}$ on its remaining original coarse words. If $n_i(w)$ counts canonical input label $i$ in the word $w$, then the resulting original law and limiting score are $$\\begin{aligned}\n q_i&=\\frac1b\\sum_{s,t}a_s r_{st}\\,\n                   \\mathbb E_{w\\sim P_{st}}n_i(w),\n       \\label{alg:recipe-law}\\\\\n u&=\\frac1b\\left(u_0+\\sum_s a_su_s\n                     +\\sum_{s,t}a_s r_{st}H(P_{st})\\right).\n       \\label{alg:recipe-score}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The formula is feasible for every stipulated finite collection of child recipes and rational distributions; no optimality assertion is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-107.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

For completion this label is deterministic and $u_s=0$. For parity it is the other of the majority and odd-count labels. For each permitted $(s,t)$, choose a rational distribution $P_{st}$ on its remaining original coarse words. If $n_i(w)$ counts canonical input label $i$ in the word $w$, then the resulting original law and limiting score are $$\\begin{aligned}\n q_i&=\\frac1b\\sum_{s,t}a_s r_{st}\\,\n                   \\mathbb E_{w\\sim P_{st}}n_i(w),\n       \\label{alg:recipe-law}\\\\\n u&=\\frac1b\\left(u_0+\\sum_s a_su_s\n                     +\\sum_{s,t}a_s r_{st}H(P_{st})\\right).\n       \\label{alg:recipe-score}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The formula is feasible for every stipulated finite collection of child recipes and rational distributions; no optimality assertion is required.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-val-dilution",
        "exact_quote": "If a construction or a convex mixture has law $\\widehat\\alpha$, and a desired law $\\alpha$ has positive coordinates, put $$\\lambda=\\max_{s:\\widehat\\alpha_s>0}\n \\left(1-\\frac{\\alpha_s}{\\widehat\\alpha_s}\\right)_+.$$ For $\\lambda>0$, the vector $r=(\\alpha-(1-\\lambda)\\widehat\\alpha)/\\lambda$ is a probability law. Mixing the construction with singleton constructions of average law $r$ therefore gives $\\alpha$ exactly, at loss at most $K\\lambda$ if the old score is at most $K$, where $K\\ge0$. Moreover $$\\lambda\\le\n \\max_s\\frac{(\\widehat\\alpha_s-\\alpha_s)_+}{\\alpha_s}.$$ When the data are rational, this is a finite rational time-sharing operation. For limiting data it is justified by the defining closure. This correction will be used when converting approximate finite laws to exact target laws.",
        "source_id": "PM-107.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-val-recompile",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-face",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\alpha=(p_0,p_1,p_2,E_0,E_1,E_2)\\in\\Delta(\\mathcal S)$ satisfies $E_1=0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-ap",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\beta\\leq3$, and let $\\alpha(\\mu)$ be a $C^2$ chart of strictly positive six-state laws, parametrized by their means on an open subset of $\\{\\mu:\\sum_i\\mu_i=2\\}$. Suppose that a $C^2$ function $\\varphi$ touches $F_\\beta\\circ\\alpha$ from below at an interior point $\\mu_*$. If $g_*$ is any supporting supergradient of $F_\\beta$ at $\\alpha(\\mu_*)$, then $$\\label{ap:lower-test}\n \\mathcal L\\varphi(\\mu_*)\n \\leq g_*\\cdot\\mathcal L\\alpha(\\mu_*)-1.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-107.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compile",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite horizon $n$, a finite set of disjoint shared terminal windows $J$, and a rational target law $a$. At every target and prefix node $v=(j,h)$, prescribe an earlier finite strong realization with exact canonical law $q_v$, utility $u_v$, and uniform auxiliary and byproduct dimensions. Assume that every path permitted by these laws belongs to its target windows. Put $$w_{j,h}=a_j\\prod_{t=1}^{|h|}q_{j,h_{<t}}(h_t),\n \\qquad\n \\overline q=\\frac1n\\sum_{|h|<n,\\ j\\in J}w_{j,h}q_{j,h}.$$ Then finite strong realizations with exact law $\\overline q$ attain, in the limit of their outer bank sizes, $$\\label{ap:compile-score}\n u_{\\mathrm{out}}\n \\geq\\frac{\\lambda H(a)}n\n      +\\frac1n\\sum_{|h|<n,\\ j\\in J}w_{j,h}u_{j,h}.$$ The limiting notation only removes a multinomial type-count loss; every member of the family is a finite tensor construction using one fixed auxiliary tensor and nonzero counted outputs.",
        "source_id": "PM-107.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compile",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite horizon $n$, a finite set of disjoint shared terminal windows $J$, and a rational target law $a$. At every target and prefix node $v=(j,h)$, prescribe an earlier finite strong realization with exact canonical law $q_v$, utility $u_v$, and uniform auxiliary and byproduct dimensions. Assume that every path permitted by these laws belongs to its target windows. Put $$w_{j,h}=a_j\\prod_{t=1}^{|h|}q_{j,h_{<t}}(h_t),\n \\qquad\n \\overline q=\\frac1n\\sum_{|h|<n,\\ j\\in J}w_{j,h}q_{j,h}.$$ Then finite strong realizations with exact law $\\overline q$ attain, in the limit of their outer bank sizes, $$\\label{ap:compile-score}\n u_{\\mathrm{out}}\n \\geq\\frac{\\lambda H(a)}n\n      +\\frac1n\\sum_{|h|<n,\\ j\\in J}w_{j,h}u_{j,h}.$$ The limiting notation only removes a multinomial type-count loss; every member of the family is a finite tensor construction using one fixed auxiliary tensor and nonzero counted outputs.",
          "source_id": "PM-107.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a finite horizon $n$, a finite set of disjoint shared terminal windows $J$, and a rational target law $a$. At every target and prefix node $v=(j,h)$, prescribe an earlier finite strong realization with exact canonical law $q_v$, utility $u_v$, and uniform auxiliary and byproduct dimensions. Assume that every path permitted by these laws belongs to its target windows. Put $$w_{j,h}=a_j\\prod_{t=1}^{|h|}q_{j,h_{<t}}(h_t),\n \\qquad\n \\overline q=\\frac1n\\sum_{|h|<n,\\ j\\in J}w_{j,h}q_{j,h}.$$ Then finite strong realizations with exact law $\\overline q$ attain, in the limit of their outer bank sizes, $$\\label{ap:compile-score}\n u_{\\mathrm{out}}\n \\geq\\frac{\\lambda H(a)}n\n      +\\frac1n\\sum_{|h|<n,\\ j\\in J}w_{j,h}u_{j,h}.$$ The limiting notation only removes a multinomial type-count loss; every member of the family is a finite tensor construction using one fixed auxiliary tensor and nonzero counted outputs.",
      "explanation": "A finite control tree of strong realizations compiles into one realization with the weighted utility plus target entropy.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ap-corridor",
        "exact_quote": "Assume that every $|m|\\leq m_0$ is available as a soft mean, with cost at most $|m|^2/2$, bounded increments $|X|\\leq B$, and the limit <a href=\"#ap:noise-limit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ap:noise-limit\">[ap:noise-limit]</a>. Assume also the singleton controls above. Fix $c>0$ and a target bound $C_y>0$. For every $\\eta>0$ there is a constant $K_\\eta$, independent of $L$, with the following property. For each integer $L\\geq2$, all sufficiently large finite horizons $n$, and every target $y$ with $|y|\\leq C_y\\sqrt n$, there is a finite control tree such that every permitted path satisfies $$\\left|\\sum_{t=1}^n X_t-y\\right|<w,\n \\qquad w=c\\sqrt n/L,$$ and its expected total cost is at most $$\\label{ap:total-cost}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{t=1}^n c_t\\leq(\\tau+2\\eta)\\log L+K_\\eta.$$ The expectation is only an accounting device for the tree’s transition laws; the terminal inclusion holds for every path.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ap-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega$ be a bounded domain in the mean plane with compact closure, and let a chart $\\alpha$ as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:ap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:ap\">26</a> extend continuously to $\\overline\\Omega$.         "exact_quote": "Suppose $0\\leq h\\leq K$ is a concave value on the full source simplex, $\\alpha$ is an interior law, and $g$ is a supporting supergradient there. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Cost of a tensor** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Saying that $T$ has *cost* $R$ means that $T$, up to a nonzero scalar, is the first nonzero coefficient of a polynomial or Laurent polynomial tensor family with a rank-$R$ decomposition.
- **W-partition** ([source](#alg:five)): We call any such three-group partition a *W-partition*; the three predicates, rather than the original numerical indices, are its one-hot flags.
- **Orientation bank** ([source](#pm-h-17)): For $\sigma\in\mathfrak S_3$, the *orientation bank* $\sigma$ is the collection of original occurrences with that net permutation relative to their canonical coordinates.
- **Strong banked realization** ([source](#stmt-def-strong)): A strong realization consists of a finite auxiliary tensor $\mathcal A$, a finite degeneration by three local maps, a set $\Lambda$ of direct labels, and positive integers $a,b,c$, with the following properties. 1. Every $\lambda\in\Lambda$ designates an original word $w(\lambda)$, and $\lambda\mapsto w(\lambda)$ is injective. All these words have the same exact canonical law in each orientation bank. 2. For every family of unresolved tensors $P=(P_w)$ the same maps give $$\mathcal T(P)\otimes\mathcal A \unrhd \bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Lambda} \bigl(P_{w(\lambda)}\otimes\langle a,b,c\rangle\bigr). \label{val:strong-identity}$$ In this display the original local coordinates of $w(\lambda)$ are retained as well; only their fixed basis factors are suppressed. Original coefficients and unresolved local indices are unchanged. 3. The auxiliary input, its charged border-rank bound, and the output dimensions $a,b,c$ are independent of $\lambda$. The identity is required also for zero $P_w$. Such an output is a formal zero fiber and contributes no nonzero direct summand. A realization used in an attainable-value calculation must separately establish that all its counted fibers are nonzero on its specified input.
- **Attainable-value functions** ([source](#stmt-val-def-value)): For each finite realization let $\alpha$ be its canonical bank law. Take all pairs $(\alpha,t)$ with $t$ no greater than its score, and take their ordinary Euclidean closure in $\Delta(\mathcal B)\times\mathbb R$. The upper boundaries of these closed sets are denoted by $F_\beta(\alpha)$ for the six-state class and $U^W_\gamma(q)$ for the $\mathsf W$ class.
- **Readable label tree** ([source](#stmt-def-label-tree)): A *readable label tree* for the support of a tensor is a finite rooted partition tree of the support whose leaves are in bijection with the supported monomials. Each monomial follows exactly one path. At each nonterminal node, conditional on the path to that node, the next child is a function of either of two specified side coordinates separately. The reader pair may vary with the node.
- **Completion of size m** ([source](#stmt-def-completion)): A completion of size $m\ge2$ with center $i$ takes $m$ copies of a three-flag tensor and retains the color words in which at most one distinct noncenter color occurs. A pure-center word has new color $i$; otherwise its new color is the unique noncenter color $j$ that occurs.
- **Finite family and support function** ([source](#stmt-def-support-function)): Let $\mathcal F_7$ contain both bases, every list of at most seven completions with sizes $2$ or $3$ and arbitrary centers, every termination pair, and every distinguished side $L$. Define $$\label{eq:F-definition} F(t)=\max_{\mathcal F_7}\frac{D_{ij}(t)}{b\log R}.$$ For an available square exponent estimate $p\ge w(1)$, define $$F_p(t)=\max\{F(t),(2+t)/p\}.$$
- **Table baseline** ([source](#stmt-def-baseline)): For $p>2$, the table baseline $\mathcal B_p$ is generated by: 1. the numerical entries of Table 1 of (Vassilevska Williams et al. 2023; Alman et al. 2025) [[vxxz](#ref-vxxz), [advxxz](#ref-advxxz)], with the exponent-two endpoint entered as $(1,0.321334,1;2)$; 2. the square input $(1,1,1;p)$; 3. the naive matrix-multiplication estimates. We close these inputs under permutations, scaling, tensor products, padding, ordinary blocking and convex interpolation. Ordinary subdivisions into products covering an index box may also be used. More precise input values may be substituted if their separator tests below are verified.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-rank-exponent** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-exponent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-exponent): Recursive blocking, division elimination, flattening. -> exponent as a rank limit
- **s-border-overhead** [claim](#stmt-lem-border-overhead) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-border-overhead): Univariate interpolation. -> exact rank bound for powers
- **s-rectangular-sum** [claim](#stmt-lem-rectangular-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rectangular-sum): Batching requests through the power of the direct sum. -> exponent bound by log cost minus log multiplicity
- **s-cw-rank** [claim](#stmt-alg-cw-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-cw-rank): Root-of-unity filtering identity. -> border rank three
- **s-asi** [claim](#stmt-thm-asi) / [proof](#stmt-thm-asi): Cited asymptotic sum inequality. -> volume-power inequality
- **s-separator** [claim](#stmt-lem-separator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separator): Sphere pigeonhole and group tensor with local projections. -> direct sum of pairings
- **s-payload** [claim](#stmt-alg-payload) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-payload): Coefficientwise reading of surviving triples. -> fiber preservation
- **s-hierarchy** [claim](#stmt-lem-hierarchy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hierarchy): Exact types and chain rule. -> entropy-order resources
- **s-completion** [claim](#stmt-alg-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-completion): Local predicates and monomial degeneration. -> new W-partition
- **s-elementary** [claim](#stmt-thm-elementary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-elementary): Completion plus complete label hierarchy. -> explicit square bound
- **s-recipes** [claim](#stmt-alg-recipe-composition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-recipe-composition): Compose outer and conditional recipes with exact types. -> law and score formulas
- **s-values** [claim](#stmt-prop-values) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-values): Entropy caps, mixing, closure. -> concave continuous values
- **s-monotonicity** [claim](#stmt-prop-monotonicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-monotonicity): Recompilation on long pools. -> monotone value
- **s-matching-face** [claim](#stmt-prop-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-matching): Progression-free hashing and isolated edges. -> marginal lower bound
- **s-lower-test** [claim](#stmt-thm-ap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ap-corridor-2): Contradiction via corridor control and compilation. -> lower-test inequality
- **s-certificate** [claim](#stmt-thm-certificate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-boundary-lemma): Barrier comparison with interior and boundary margins. -> value above log 3
- **s-square-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-rectangular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-boundary-lemma-2): Select finite construction and apply asymptotic sum inequality. -> strict square bound
- **s-entropy** [claim](#stmt-thm-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-entropy): Node-by-node separation with multinomial counts. -> exponent at reader rates
- **s-shape** [claim](#stmt-thm-shape-optimization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-shape-optimization): Support function over completion scripts. -> one-parameter maximization
- **s-char-transfer** [claim](#stmt-cor-characteristic-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-characteristic-transfer): Nullstellensatz descent and specialization. -> bounds outside a finite prime set

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-completion-component-count**: For block length b at least 2, after completing b copies of the five-term degeneration and deleting the all-A group, the number of retained original scalar components equals 4^b+3^b-2^{b+1}. For b equal to 2, 3 and 4 this gives 17, 75 and 305.
- **inv-completion-exponent-bound**: The exponent bound is 3b log 3 divided by log of the retained component count, evaluated at the count 4^b+3^b-2^{b+1}.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-monotone-in-b**: Assume that a larger completion block length always yields a better exponent bound and pick b as large as affordable. -> The bound is not monotone in b: the block length three gives a smaller value than lengths two and four.
- **anti-implementation-claim**: Treat the exponent bound as evidence that a practical fast matrix-multiplication routine of that exponent can be run on real inputs. -> The bound is an asymptotic arithmetic exponent; nothing is asserted about the size of a useful finite implementation or its numerical stability.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Theorem 11 is a finite explicit construction: the retained components are an explicit finite set of coarse words weighted by group sizes, and the exponent bound is an explicit closed-form function of their count, so both can be computed and independently checked by enumeration.

```python
# INPUTS: one integer b (block length) on stdin
# PRECONDITIONS: b is an integer with 2 <= b <= 40
# OUTPUTS: one line: retained component count s_b, then the bound 3*b*log(3)/log(s_b) with 6 decimals
# TIME: O(b) big-integer operations
# SPACE: O(b) bits
# ARITHMETIC: exact integers for the count; double precision natural logarithm for the bound
# FAILURE: non-integer or out-of-range input raises an exception and a nonzero exit status
import math
b = int(input().split()[0])
s = 4**b+3**b-2**(b+1)
print(s, "%.6f" % (3*b*math.log(3)/math.log(s)))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an asymptotic arithmetic exponent in an operation-count model. The paper gives no implementation, and it states that the finite certificate is not a practical matrix-multiplication implementation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No simulation, physics, or game mechanic appears. The only dynamics are auxiliary control trees inside a proof, with no runtime meaning. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The checker for the finite certificate is a one-off verifier tied to this paper's coefficients and thresholds. It is not a reusable tool. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A fixed exact rank scheme for a small matrix-product tensor yields a recursive block algorithm whose operation count follows from the rank budget. The paper uses this only to define the exponent, not as a recommended routine. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper builds tensors and label trees as proof objects. It defines no data layout, index, or access operation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Only abstract arithmetic counts are considered, with no memory, bandwidth, or parallelism. The paper disclaims practical crossover sizes. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No cryptographic primitive, hardness assumption, or security claim occurs in the paper. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem or data model occurs. Matrix multiplication is treated purely as a bilinear map. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | For a fixed finite family of scripts, the best rectangular bound at a given aspect ratio reduces to minimizing a convex support function ratio over one parameter in a closed interval. This is a finite, computable optimization. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, consensus, or concurrency model occurs. Field-transfer discussion is about algebraic characteristic, not about nodes. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main contribution is an existential-asymptotic bound whose scope excludes positive-characteristic dual-exponent transfer and the exceptional primes, so no further engineering domain is supported. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-complex-field-scope** (hypothesis): The headline exponent bounds are proved for tensors and arithmetic over the complex numbers, in the arithmetic-operation model that counts field operations. They are not claimed in any other ring, semiring or cost model. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated exponents to Boolean, tropical or other semiring matrix products', 'Reading the bounds as bit-complexity or word-RAM running times']
- **limit-exceptional-primes-uncomputed** (nonconstructive): The transfer to positive characteristic holds outside one finite set of primes that is obtained abstractly from a chosen maximal ideal and is never computed. For a given prime it is therefore unknown whether the bound applies. Blocked use: ['Asserting the strict square or rectangular bound over a specific finite field without separately excluding the exceptional set', 'Using the corollary as an effective procedure that lists the allowed characteristics']
- **limit-no-positive-characteristic-dual** (formal_scope): The dual-exponent bound is established only over fields of characteristic zero, because its witnesses depend on the accuracy parameter and give no common finite set of exceptional primes. Blocked use: ['Quoting the dual-exponent lower bound over a field of positive characteristic', 'Combining the characteristic-zero dual corollary with the finite-exception corollary to conclude a positive-characteristic dual bound']
- **limit-no-crossover-or-implementation** (edge_case): The result is an asymptotic arithmetic exponent with arbitrarily small positive slack. Nothing is said about the matrix size at which the constructions beat classical methods, about implementation constants, or about numerical stability. Blocked use: ['Deploying the constructions as a production multiplication routine for finite sizes', 'Using the exponent as evidence of floating-point accuracy or stability']
- **limit-cost-is-upper-bound-witness** (approximation): The cost of a tensor is a supplied polynomial witness and a certified upper bound, not the border rank itself. Rank budgets in the constructions are therefore charges for specific witnesses and need not be minimal. Blocked use: ['Treating a stated cost as the exact border rank of the tensor', 'Deriving lower bounds on border rank from the budgets used in the constructions']
- **limit-fiber-nonvanishing** (hypothesis): The separation identity is a locality statement. When an earlier support restriction makes a designated word absent, its fiber is zero and cannot be counted as an output, so every counted output needs a separate nonvanishing argument. Blocked use: ['Counting every direct summand of a separation identity as a nonzero output without a support argument', 'Applying the identity inside a restricted tensor and assuming the original number of words survives']
- **limit-completion-block-nonmonotone** (edge_case): The elementary completion bound depends on the block length and is not monotone in it. The best value among the tabulated lengths occurs at the middle length, and no optimization over all restrictions of the power is claimed. Blocked use: ['Choosing the block length as large as possible to improve the exponent', 'Presenting the three-block construction as optimal among all completions']
- **limit-arithmetic-exponent-only** (formal_scope): The square-bound argument establishes an arithmetic exponent through a finite construction selected from a closure of attainable scores. It does not estimate the size of a useful finite implementation. Blocked use: ['Reading the certificate as an explicit algorithm with known block sizes and group sizes', 'Using the strict score gain to bound the dimension at which the construction first helps']
- **limit-envelope-assumption-untabulated** (hypothesis): The extension of the baseline comparison to further old estimates holds only under the stated envelope condition, and the published record values alone do not establish that condition for untabulated parameter choices. Blocked use: ['Claiming strict improvement over every possible enlarged baseline without verifying the envelope condition', 'Adding new estimates to the table baseline and quoting the improvement interval unchanged']
- **limit-certificate-not-formalized** (formal_scope): The finite numerical certificate is verified by reproducible integer and rational programs, not by a proof-assistant formalization. The continuum conclusion rests on the analytic error allowances of the paper. Blocked use: ['Citing the result as machine-checked in a proof assistant', 'Treating a passing run of the checker as a replacement for the interpolation and error-allowance arguments']

Scope note of family 107 (`lean/docs/107.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Complex Matrix Multiplication Below 2.258 and Rectangular Bounds](../../preprints/Complex-Matrix-Multiplication-Below-2.258-and-Rectangular-Bounds-September-24-2026/Complex-Matrix-Multiplication-Below-2.258-and-Rectangular-Bounds-September-24-2026.pdf)
> - [Staggered extraction for exact matrix multiplication over every field](../../preprints/Staggered-extraction-for-exact-matrix-multiplication-over-every-field-September-24-2026/Staggered-extraction-for-exact-matrix-multiplication-over-every-field-September-24-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized results bound the complex matrix-multiplication exponent by $\omega(\mathbb C)\le9/4$, the dual exponent by $\alpha>0.465$, and the rectangular exponent at aspect ratio $0.709$ by $\omega(\mathbb C;1,0.709,1)<2.092$. The dual exponent is the supremum of rectangular aspect ratios attainable with exponent $2$. The arithmetic model counts additions, subtractions, and multiplications in finite division-free programs, with arbitrary positive exponent slack. The square bound implies the paper's weaker $2.258$ headline bound.
>
> The formalized result gives the unconditional bound $\omega(F)<2.371054886006746$ for every field $F$, including finite fields and fields of positive characteristic. The exponent counts additions, subtractions, and multiplications in finite division-free arithmetic programs, with arbitrary positive exponent slack. No numerical inequalities remain as hypotheses. The result concerns arithmetic complexity, rather than bit complexity or practical crossover sizes.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! The three main complex matrix multiplication bounds, checked independently. -/

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators

namespace MatrixMultiplication.Arithmetic

inductive Gate (F Input Register : Type*) where
  | constant : F → Gate F Input Register
  | input : Input → Gate F Input Register
  | add : Register → Register → Gate F Input Register
  | sub : Register → Register → Gate F Input Register
  | mul : Register → Register → Gate F Input Register

namespace Gate

variable {F Input Register : Type*}

def eval [Field F] (inputs : Input → F) (registers : Register → F) :
    Gate F Input Register → F
  | .constant z => z
  | .input i => inputs i
  | .add i j => registers i + registers j
  | .sub i j => registers i - registers j
  | .mul i j => registers i * registers j

def cost : Gate F Input Register → ℕ
  | .constant _ => 0
  | .input _ => 0
  | .add _ _ => 1
  | .sub _ _ => 1
  | .mul _ _ => 1

end Gate

inductive Program (F Input : Type*) : ℕ → Type _ where
  | nil : Program F Input 0
  | step {r : ℕ} : Program F Input r → Gate F Input (Fin r) → Program F Input (r + 1)

namespace Program

variable {F Input : Type*}

def eval [Field F] : {r : ℕ} → Program F Input r → (Input → F) → Fin r → F
  | 0, .nil, _ => Fin.elim0
  | _ + 1, .step p g, inputs =>
    Fin.cases (g.eval inputs (p.eval inputs)) (p.eval inputs)

def cost : {r : ℕ} → Program F Input r → ℕ
  | 0, .nil => 0
  | _ + 1, .step p g => p.cost + g.cost

end Program

abbrev MatrixInput (a b c : ℕ) := (Fin a × Fin b) ⊕ (Fin b × Fin c)

def matrixInputs {F : Type*} {a b c : ℕ}
    (A : Matrix (Fin a) (Fin b) F) (B : Matrix (Fin b) (Fin c) F) :
    MatrixInput a b c → F
  | .inl (i, j) => A i j
  | .inr (j, k) => B j k

structure MatrixAlgorithm (F : Type*) (a b c : ℕ) where
  registers : ℕ
  program : Program F (MatrixInput a b c) registers
  output : Fin a → Fin c → Fin registers

namespace MatrixAlgorithm

variable {F : Type*} [Field F] {a b c : ℕ}

def eval (P : MatrixAlgorithm F a b c)
    (A : Matrix (Fin a) (Fin b) F) (B : Matrix (Fin b) (Fin c) F) :
    Matrix (Fin a) (Fin c) F :=
  fun i k => P.program.eval (matrixInputs A B) (P.output i k)

def Correct (P : MatrixAlgorithm F a b c) : Prop :=
  ∀ (A : Matrix (Fin a) (Fin b) F) (B : Matrix (Fin b) (Fin c) F), P.eval A B = A * B

def cost (P : MatrixAlgorithm F a b c) : ℕ := P.program.cost

end MatrixAlgorithm

/-- Positive slack, one uniform constant, and a correct program at every size. -/
def AdmissibleExponent (F : Type*) [Field F] (τ : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ C : ℝ, 0 < C ∧
    ∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n → ∃ P : MatrixAlgorithm F n n n,
      P.Correct ∧ (P.cost : ℝ) ≤ C * (n : ℝ) ^ (τ + ε)

def omega (F : Type*) [Field F] : ℝ := sInf {τ : ℝ | AdmissibleExponent F τ}

/-- The inner dimension of an `n × n^k` by `n^k × n` multiplication. -/
def innerSize (n : ℕ) (k : ℝ) : ℕ := ⌈(n : ℝ) ^ k⌉₊

def RectangularAdmissibleExponent (F : Type*) [Field F] (k τ : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ C : ℝ, 0 < C ∧
    ∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n → ∃ P : MatrixAlgorithm F n (innerSize n k) n,
      P.Correct ∧ (P.cost : ℝ) ≤ C * (n : ℝ) ^ (τ + ε)

def rectangularOmega (F : Type*) [Field F] (k : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sInf {τ : ℝ | RectangularAdmissibleExponent F k τ}

/-- The dual exponent is a supremum of exact exponent-two rectangular shapes. -/
def complexAlpha : ℝ :=
  sSup {k : ℝ | k ∈ Set.Icc 0 1 ∧ rectangularOmega ℂ k = 2}

end MatrixMultiplication.Arithmetic

end

namespace MatrixMultiplication

theorem complex_omega_le_nine_quarters :
    Arithmetic.omega ℂ ≤ (9 : ℝ) / 4 := by
  sorry

theorem complex_alpha_gt_93_div_200 :
    (93 : ℝ) / 200 < Arithmetic.complexAlpha := by
  sorry

theorem complex_rectangular_omega_lt_523_div_250 :
    Arithmetic.rectangularOmega ℂ ((709 : ℝ) / 1000) < (523 : ℝ) / 250 := by
  sorry

end MatrixMultiplication

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


— secondary writeup

Over every field of characteristic zero, we prove that the square matrix-multiplication exponent satisfies <i>ω</i> &lt; 2.258, the dual exponent satisfies <i>α</i> &gt; 0.465, and $`\omega(1,0.709,1)\lt 2.092`$. The strict square and <i>k</i> = 0.709 rectangular bounds also hold over every field except possibly in one finite set of positive characteristics, in the arithmetic-operation model.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Matrix multiplication asks for the bilinear map $(A,B)\mapsto AB$, where the two input matrices have compatible sizes. Its arithmetic complexity measures the number of field operations needed to compute all entries of the product. Except in the field-transfer results of Section <a href="#sec:characteristic-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:characteristic-transfer">14</a>, the field is $\mathbb C$, and the constants in an algorithm may belong to $\mathbb C$. For $0\le k\le1$, let $$w(k)=\omega(1,k,1)$$ be the infimum of exponents for multiplying an $n\times\lceil n^k\rceil$ matrix by a $\lceil n^k\rceil\times n$ matrix. The bound $w(k)\le\tau$ means that for every $\varepsilon>0$ these products can be computed using $O(n^{\tau+\varepsilon})$ arithmetic operations. The square exponent is $\omega=w(1)$, and the dual exponent is $$\alpha=\sup\{k\in[0,1]:w(k)=2\}.$$ The output size gives $w(k)\ge2$. Thus $\alpha$ measures how far the inner dimension can grow while multiplication retains the exponent of its output size. Square and rectangular multiplication are basic operations in algebraic algorithms; controlling the rectangular shape can matter even when a square bound is already available.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and main result

Strassen’s rank-seven algorithm for $2\times2$ multiplication gave $\omega\le\log_2 7$, establishing subcubic arithmetic complexity (Strassen 1969) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[strassen](#ref-strassen)]. Approximate bilinear algorithms and their conversion to exact algorithms (Bini 1980) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[bini](#ref-bini)], Schönhage’s asymptotic sum inequality (Schönhage 1981) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[schonhage](#ref-schonhage)], and Strassen’s laser method (Strassen 1987) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[strassen-laser](#ref-strassen-laser)] made tensor powers and degenerations central to subsequent advances. Coppersmith and Winograd combined low-border-rank tensors with arithmetic-progression constructions (Coppersmith and Winograd 1990) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[cw](#ref-cw)]. Their extraction method restricts powers of a small tensor to direct sums of matrix products, whose dimensions enter the asymptotic sum inequality; their tensor-square analysis gave $\omega<2.375477$ (Coppersmith and Winograd 1990, sec. 8) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[cw](#ref-cw)]. Higher powers and more detailed analyses of these tensors led to the improvements of Stothers, Vassilevska Williams, and Le Gall (Stothers 2010; Vassilevska Williams 2012; Le Gall 2014) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[stothers](#ref-stothers), [williams-powers](#ref-williams-powers), [legall-powers](#ref-legall-powers)].

Rectangular multiplication requires separate control of the three matrix dimensions. Early work of Coppersmith and of Lotti and Romani developed this direction (Coppersmith 1982; Lotti and Romani 1983) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[coppersmith1982](#ref-coppersmith1982), [lottiromani1983](#ref-lottiromani1983)]; later constructions of Coppersmith, Le Gall, and Le Gall and Urrutia improved the available rectangular and dual bounds (Coppersmith 1997; Le Gall 2012; Le Gall and Urrutia 2018) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[coppersmith-rect](#ref-coppersmith-rect), [legall-rect](#ref-legall-rect), [legall-urrutia](#ref-legall-urrutia)]. Recent advances include the refined laser method of Alman and Vassilevska Williams (Alman and Vassilevska Williams 2024) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[alman-williams](#ref-alman-williams)], the asymmetric hashing of Duan, Wu, and Zhou (Duan, Wu, and Zhou 2023) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[duan-wu-zhou](#ref-duan-wu-zhou)], and the square and rectangular analyses of Vassilevska Williams, Xu, Xu, and Zhou (Vassilevska Williams et al. 2023) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[vxxz](#ref-vxxz)] <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[advxxz](#ref-advxxz)] and Alman, Duan, Vassilevska Williams, Xu, Xu, and Zhou (Alman et al. 2025). The four-author work establishes $\alpha\ge0.321334$; these two papers also give detailed tables of rectangular estimates. Dupont et al. subsequently optimized the asymmetric framework at a larger scale to obtain $\omega<2.371177$ (Dupont et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[dupont](#ref-dupont)], a bound incorporated into the updated table of (Alman et al. 2025) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[advxxz](#ref-advxxz)].

The common extraction problem is to separate many candidate products that share variables, while accounting for the cost of every operation used to separate them. We adjoin auxiliary group tensors that both resolve shared labels and supply useful matrix-product factors. Their full rank cost enters the resulting inequalities. This construction gives the following bounds.

<a id="stmt-thm-rectangular"></a>
<div id="thm:rectangular" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *<span id="thm:main" label="thm:main"></span><span id="thm:square" label="thm:square"></span>Over $\mathbb C$, the arithmetic matrix-multiplication exponents satisfy $$\omega<\frac{1129}{500}=2.258,\qquad
 \alpha>0.465,\qquad w(0.709)<2.092.$$*

</div>

Corollary <a href="#cor:characteristic-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:characteristic-transfer">52</a> transfers the strict square and $k=0.709$ inequalities to every field of characteristic zero or outside one finite set of positive characteristics, in the same arithmetic-operation model. It selects two fixed exact rank schemes, realizes their coefficients over one number field, and specializes their polynomial identities. The exceptional primes are not computed. Corollary <a href="#cor:characteristic-zero-dual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:characteristic-zero-dual">53</a> also gives the same dual-exponent bound over every field of characteristic zero. No dual-exponent transfer in positive characteristic is asserted.

The square bound also lowers the arithmetic exponent of basic linear-algebra tasks. Given $A\in\mathbb C^{n\times n}$ and $b\in\mathbb C^n$, one can compute $\det A$ and, when $A$ is nonsingular, $A^{-1}$ and the solution of $Ax=b$, using $O(n^c)$ field operations for some fixed $c<2.258$, with exact zero tests for pivot selection. Choose $\omega<c<2.258$ and apply the fast elimination reductions of Strassen (Strassen 1969) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[strassen](#ref-strassen)] and Ibarra, Moran, and Hui (Ibarra, Moran, and Hui 1982, Theorem 2.1 and Corollary 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[ibarra-moran-hui](#ref-ibarra-moran-hui)].

Two applications of the same separation lemma prove the theorem. The square bound uses finite tensor constructions, a differential comparison for their attainable values, and an exact numerical certificate. The dual and rectangular bounds come from explicit conditional-label trees and rational entropy certificates. We also optimize all leaf probability laws in a specified finite family of such trees, obtaining a bound for every aspect ratio in $[0,1]$. The square and rectangular construction families, and the precise way a square estimate augments the latter, are described below.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Separating labels with an auxiliary tensor

A trilinear tensor is a sum of monomials $xyz$, with variables drawn from three finite coordinate sets $X,Y,Z$. Its rank is the least number of products of three linear forms needed to express it. A restriction substitutes linear forms separately on the three sides. A degeneration allows parameter-dependent substitutions and takes a leading coefficient. Border rank is the least rank of tensors approaching the given tensor. These operations are local: a coordinate substitution cannot depend on information visible only on another side.

Suppose that the supported monomials carry $K$ labels, and that each of two sides can independently determine the label from its own coordinate. The third side may still share variables between labels. The pairwise separation lemma, <a href="#lem:group" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:group">[lem:group]</a>, tensors the input with a finite abelian group-addition tensor of rank $K^{1+o(1)}$. Local projections then give a direct sum over the labels, with a size-$K$ dot-product factor on the two original readers in every summand. The label is now visible on all three sides. Dot-product factors on the three possible reader pairs combine into a matrix multiplication tensor.

The construction combines Fourier diagonalization of the group tensor with tags of equal Euclidean norm and a separate constant-inner-product slice for each tag. Its geometry adapts Behrend’s construction (Behrend 1946) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[behrend](#ref-behrend)], following the progression-free constructions of Salem and Spencer (Salem and Spencer 1942) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[salem-spencer](#ref-salem-spencer)]. The use of group algebras also has an established place in the approach of Cohn and Umans (Cohn and Umans 2003) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[cohn-umans](#ref-cohn-umans)]; the local separation identity needed here is proved in full. It preserves every original coordinate and coefficient, including additional tensor indices correlated across occurrences. Consequently the same identity can be applied inside a previously restricted tensor. A separate support argument ensures that every summand counted in an application is nonzero.

Successive separations are organized by exact conditional counts. Once a preceding label is direct on all three sides, each side can locate the positions on which the next operation acts. Fixing the number of each possible continuation makes the next auxiliary tensor and the output dimensions common to all branches. The multinomial counts and entropy chain rule are the standard method-of-types tools (Shannon 1948; Csiszár 1998) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[shannon1948](#ref-shannon1948), [csiszar1998](#ref-csiszar1998)]; locality and the uniform tensor accounting are proved alongside them here.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The square bound: finite constructions and comparison

The square argument starts from the six-term Coppersmith–Winograd tensor $$T=\sum_{\substack{i+j+k=2\\0\le i,j,k\le2}}x_i y_j z_k,$$ which has border rank three (Coppersmith and Winograd 1990, sec. 7, equation (10)) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[cw](#ref-cw)]. A five-term degeneration of $T$ has three groups, each detectable on one designated side. A local degeneration of three copies retains $75$ scalar components. Separating their successive labels gives the elementary estimate $\omega\le9\log3/\log75<2.290108$ in <a href="#thm:elementary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:elementary">11</a>. This example displays the auxiliary-rank accounting before the larger construction is introduced.

For a proposed exponent $\beta$, suppose a finite construction from $n$ copies of $T$ and an auxiliary tensor of border rank at most $R$ produces $L$ independent matrix products of common dimensions $a\times b$ by $b\times c$. Its normalized score is $$\frac{\log L-\log R+(\beta/3)\log(abc)}{n}.$$ Schönhage’s asymptotic sum inequality implies that a score strictly greater than $\log3$ proves $\omega<\beta$. Thus the square argument must produce a strict score gain while retaining the entire auxiliary charge.

We organize the finite constructions by their source distribution. There are six collections of original tensor occurrences, called *orientation banks*, one for each permutation of the three sides. The distribution records the same exact frequencies in the original coordinates of each bank. This keeps a physical permutation of a tensor distinct from a change of its recorded law. The square sections use $\alpha$ for this vector-valued source law; the dual exponent in <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">[thm:main]</a> is a scalar. A construction preserves arbitrary unresolved tensors attached to its original words, and its output labels identify distinct surviving words. Taking the closure of the finite law–score pairs gives a continuous concave attainable-value function. Conditional pooling and a new application of the group separator to each longer pool provide the composition properties needed in the comparison.

The key analytic step uses restrictions on sums of the local grades $i,j,k$. Each side can read its own sum. Windows placed at progression-free target indices force the three locally read targets to agree, creating another direct label. The progression-free construction and the matching argument have the ancestry of Behrend and Coppersmith–Winograd (Behrend 1946; Coppersmith and Winograd 1990) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[behrend](#ref-behrend), [cw](#ref-cw)]. To exploit the new labels, a finite-horizon control steers every word allowed by its controls into the assigned terminal window. This every-word guarantee is what permits all the resulting tensor summands to be counted. Exact rational prefix populations then translate the finite control tree into local tensor maps with common auxiliary supplies.

This construction forces a differential inequality on any smooth function touching the attainable value from below. The covariance of the local grades determines the weighted combination of second derivatives in this inequality. Smooth touching tests and strict comparison follow the viscosity-solution methodology of Crandall and Lions and of Crandall, Ishii, and Lions (Crandall and Lions 1983; Crandall, Ishii, and Lions 1992) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[crandalllions1983](#ref-crandalllions1983), [crandallishiilions1992](#ref-crandallishiilions1992)]; the required inequality and comparison are proved directly from the finite tensor constructions. A rational polynomial chart and a polynomial barrier then give the strict score gain at an interior source distribution. Boundary constructions supply the initial lower estimates, while explicit residual, interpolation, and rounding bounds turn the finite integer checks into inequalities on the entire comparison domain.

Finally, a strict gain in the closed attainable set selects one actual finite construction with score greater than $\log3$. Its law may be close to the chart’s central law. All control horizons, rational approximations, group sizes, and bank sizes are fixed before the asymptotic sum inequality is applied.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Rectangular bounds and finite-family optimization

The rectangular argument tracks the three matrix dimensions separately. A readable label tree partitions a tensor’s support until each leaf is one monomial; at every node, conditional on the preceding labels, the next child is independently readable on two specified sides. Fix a rational probability law on its leaves and apply the tree to an admissible number of copies with those exact leaf frequencies; arbitrary leaf laws follow by approximation. The number of child words at a node is a multinomial coefficient. The product of these coefficients is exactly the number of final direct summands, so the leading auxiliary rank charge is canceled by this multiplicity in the equal-summand inequality. The three remaining logarithmic dimensions are the conditional entropies accumulated on the three reader pairs. This is <a href="#thm:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:entropy">40</a>.

Explicit polynomial degenerations giving border-rank bounds two and three supply the initial trees. Their supports have three colors, with a designated side able to detect its own color. A *completion* takes several copies, chooses a center color, and retains color words containing at most one distinct noncenter color.
